- **citation-faithfulness**: [Activation] Use this skill when the user gives a paper PDF and asks whether its in-text citations are FAITHFUL to what the cited sources actually say — e.g. "check if this paper misrepresents its references", "does citation [12] actually support this claim", "verify the citations are not distorting the sources", "核查引用是否忠于原文", "有没有曲解参考文献". - **citation-foodchain**: Trace where an AI answer's information actually comes from. Split the answer into claims, check which cited page really supports each one, then follow every page's own sources hop by hop — press release, wire, outlet, Wikipedia, Reddit, content farm — until you reach the root. AI sources are a food chain; first-degree citations only show the last link. Also finds the exact page that introduced a wrong fact about you. - **cite-or-abstain**: Use when producing factual claims that a reader will act on - research summaries, technical answers, market or financial statements, documentation. ([Charlesganu2004/Master-Repo-Use](https://github.com/Charlesganu2004/Master-Repo-Use/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/cite-or-abstain) · 2 copies)
- **cite-or-cut**: Cite every claim a draft makes or cut it — strategy verdicts, unbounded rankings, invented frameworks, aphoristic closers, and headings that boast. Use when writing or reviewing prose, docs, commits, PR bodies, or research reports, and on "cite or cut", "check my claims", "am I overclaiming", "cut the strategy voice". ([tslateman/skills](https://github.com/tslateman/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/writing/cite-or-cut) · ★1)
- **cite-or-refuse**: Searches official documentation, source code, and other trusted sources before drawing any conclusion; cites findings with version and date; refuses to conclude when no acceptable source exists. Use this skill whenever the user signals they want a documented, verified, trusted, reliable, sourced, or evidence-backed answer — phrases like "I want a trusted answer", "back this up with sources", "cite your sources", "verified please", "don't speculate", "document this", "I need this to be reliable", or any similar request for a sourceable response. Trigger even when the topic seems familiar — the… ([Ephasme/agent-skills](https://github.com/Ephasme/agent-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/research/cite-or-refuse))
- **cite-or-silent**: Use to refactor any Vigía agent response that makes a regulatory claim into the cite-or-silent contract. Either the response carries citations[] minItems 1 with verifiable quoted_text from a canonical source, OR it returns the literal "no encontré fuente para esta consulta". Validates against the post-validator (substring + Levenshtein 0.95). Triggers - "reescribe esta respuesta con citas", "esto necesita cita", "verifica A6", "cite or silent". ([marcopavez/claude-impact-lab](https://github.com/marcopavez/claude-impact-lab/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/cite-or-silent))
- **cite-or-stop**: Use when a task is blocked, a retry failed, debugging behavior or test failures, or making factual claims about codebase/runtime state. Cite factual claims; after the same blocker twice, stop looping and hand off one next approach. Label unsourced ideas Invented (not approved). Casual and conceptual Q&A are exempt from inline tagging. ([geek-prateek/ai-coding-standards](https://github.com/geek-prateek/ai-coding-standards/tree/HEAD/cite-or-stop))
- **cite-or-unknown**: Use when writing research, Attention List, or any decision-facing claim — force verified/inferred/unknown and never invent sources. ([Shixuuu/grokbot-pi-templates](https://github.com/Shixuuu/grokbot-pi-templates/tree/HEAD/templates/community/personal-admin/hollis/skills/cite-or-unknown) · ★6 · 2 copies)
- **cite-or-unknown**: Use when writing research or any decision-facing claim — force verified/inferred/unknown and never invent sources. ([Shixuuu/grokbot-pi-templates](https://github.com/Shixuuu/grokbot-pi-templates/tree/HEAD/templates/community/research-briefings/watchdog/skills/cite-or-unknown) · ★6)
- **cite-paper**: Use this skill when the user gives a paper (arXiv ID, DOI, title, or arXiv/OpenAlex URL) and wants a citation — BibTeX, GB/T 7714, APA, or a .bib file (生成引文/导出 BibTeX/参考文献格式). Works without any API key. ([unumbrela/PaperWeave](https://github.com/unumbrela/PaperWeave/tree/HEAD/skills/cite-paper))
- **cite-placement**: Place pre-screened literature citations into a LaTeX or Word manuscript, or restyle the citations already in one. Three modes: (1) inline placement — inline \cite{}/\citet{}/\citep{} with a compiled references.bib, for author-date journals (APA, MLA, Harvard, Chicago author-date, IEEE, Vancouver); (2) footnote placement — full formatted \footnote{} or OOXML footnotes for legal and notes styles (Bluebook, OSCOLA, Chicago, APA, McGill) with Id./supra short forms; (3) restyle — convert existing footnote citations from one style to another. This skill is manual-invoke ONLY — trigger ONLY when the… ([kennethkhoocy/applied-micro-skills](https://github.com/kennethkhoocy/applied-micro-skills/tree/HEAD/plugins/applied-micro/skills/cite-placement) · ★20 · 3 copies)
- **cite-real**: Check every link cited in a Markdown file is a real readable page (not a 404, soft 404, login wall, bot check or HTML pretending to be a PDF), save the real ones, and verify citations only point at saved pages. Use for "are these links real", "check the sources", "verify citations" before publishing notes, READMEs or research. ([Dodai0419/cite-real](https://github.com/Dodai0419/cite-real/tree/HEAD/skills/cite-real))
- **cite-recon**: Survey open legal questions and research gaps in the project. Use when asked "what legal questions are open", "where are our research gaps", or "list unresolved legal issues". ([tonone-ai/tonone](https://github.com/tonone-ai/tonone/tree/HEAD/skills/cite-recon) · ★82 · 4 copies)
- **cite-references**: How to cite sources for the Book of the Dead game, both in a level's registry.json (the references dictionary and each entry's reference field) and in the Totenbuch Scene Atlas artifact (the References list and each scene's links). Úsala cuando pregunten qué norma o artículo regula algo, si algo es obligatorio o desde cuándo, o cuando otra skill necesite la cita de una afirmación legal. ([AS-prog/ai-act-advisor](https://github.com/AS-prog/ai-act-advisor/tree/HEAD/plugins/ai-act-advisor/skills/cite-regulation))
- **cite-release**: Prepare a citable release and keep its Zenodo and citation metadata correct. ([piabrota/skills](https://github.com/piabrota/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/research/cite-release))
- **cite-requirements**: Walk the operator through requirement-traceability judgement calls one at a time — flag citations (forward, D6) and requirement exclusions (reverse, D13) — then transcribe each decision into the contract YAML, the exclusions file, and a decisions journal. - **cite-research**: Legal research on a specific question — case law, statutes, regulatory guidance. - **cite-research**: Use whenever you design or build something people will use — forms, screens, game mechanics, animations, loading states, pacing — so the decision carries its research ("as per research:&lt;id&gt;, ... because ..."), and whenever a user has adjusted one feature several times in a session, to offer a search for research from reputable authorities instead of more guessing. ([8007342/tillandsias](https://github.com/8007342/tillandsias/tree/HEAD/skills/cite-research) · ★6)
- **cite-resolve**: Verify references against bibliographic registries (Crossref, DataCite, arXiv, Semantic Scholar) instead of writing DOIs, venues, volumes, pages or author lists from memory. Use whenever you are about to cite a paper, build a bibliography or BibTeX file, turn web-search results (Exa, web_search, etc., which return title and URL but no DOI) into references, or check a reference list someone gave you. Triggers: "cite", "reference list", "bibliography", "BibTeX", "DOI", "check these references", 「引用して」「参考文献」「文献リスト」「書誌を確かめて」「DOIを付けて」「bibを作って」. - **cite-review**: Use when citations in a LaTeX manuscript need checking against what the cited papers actually say — prose that may overstate, understate, or mischaracterise a source. - **cite-review**: Review a pull request or a local branch diff with the Cite reviewer, and triage what it returns. Use when asked to review a PR with Cite, or asked what a Cite run found and why. Report mode publishes nothing to GitHub, and each run writes the readable report, the JSON report, the run record and the wire capture into its own directory. ([elecnix/cite](https://github.com/elecnix/cite/tree/HEAD/skills/cite-review) · ★1)
- **cite-safely**: Writing-time discipline for any medical or scientific text that cites literature: never write a citation from memory, mark every unconfirmed reference or number with [VERIFY], label provenance ((a) what a source says, (b) the user's own data, (c) inference), flag open methodological choices with [DECIDE], and finish with the check-references then check-claims gate. Use this whenever drafting, expanding, revising or translating a manuscript, abstract, protocol, review, editorial, grant, thesis chapter, teaching slide or social post that states a study finding, statistic, effect size, guideline… ([WarsiMD/citation-guardrails](https://github.com/WarsiMD/citation-guardrails/tree/HEAD/skills/cite-safely) · ★2)

- **cite-seq-count-matrix-processing**: Use when raw ADT/protein UMI count matrices from a CITE-seq experiment are available (e.g., raw and filtered Cell Ranger outputs) and you need to characterize ambient antibody background before normalization, or verify that empty-droplet background reflects protein levels in cells. - **cite-seq-expression-normalization**: Use when raw CITE-seq or scRNA-seq count matrices (e.g., GEO GSE100866 CBMC data) must be normalized with log2(1+X) before marker selection or k-NN label classification. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cite-seq-hashtag-umi-counting))
- **cite-seq-multimodal-clustering**: Use when analyzing CITE-seq data with both transcriptome and surface-protein (ADT) modalities and the goal is joint cell-population clustering plus quantitative purity scoring in both modalities. - **cite-seq-multimodal-distance-fusion**: Use when analyzing CITE-seq data with a limited number of ADT features (e.g. &lt;50) where unimodal or naive joint analysis fails to separate subsets (e.g. memory vs naive CD4+ T cells), or when distinguishing antigen-specific B cell populations ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cite-seq-multimodal-distance-fusion))
- **cite-seq-multimodal-embedding-generation**: Use when CITE-seq or other multimodal single-cell data (same cells measured across modalities, e.g. RNA + ADT matrices) needs integrated embeddings to cluster cells into major and minor cell types. - **cite-seq-multimodal-integration**: Use when evaluating or reproducing multimodal CITE-seq integration, specifically testing whether ALRA imputation of RNA counts improves agreement between RNA-space and ADT protein-space pairwise cell distances. - **cite-seq-multimodal-reference-building**: Use when a CITE-seq reference with paired mRNA and surface protein measurements is available but query samples are mRNA-only, and you need to infer query surface protein levels from reference mapping. - **cite-seq-protein-differential-activity-analysis**: Use when analyzing a multimodal CITE-seq/PBMC h5ad atlas (&gt;200k cells, 228 proteins) to rank surface proteins by differential activity in PCA space without clustering, and to compare that ranking against cluster-based DE baselines. - **cite-seq-protein-distance-constraint-generation**: Use when a CITE-seq dataset provides protein (ADT) measurements on the same cells as mRNA counts, and the goal is to improve mRNA clustering accuracy or enforce biologically consistent cluster labels (e.g., CD4/CD8 T cell subtypes) via pairwise constraints. - **cite-seq-protein-imputation-from-scrna**: Use when you have a UMI-based scRNA-seq count matrix and want per-cell relative abundance estimates of surface protein immunophenotype markers, or when benchmarking imputation against raw/denoised RNA baselines or Seurat v3 anchor transfer. - **cite-seq-protein-mrna-correlation-analysis**: Use when evaluating whether a denoising/imputation method improves scRNA-seq data using CITE-seq ADT protein abundances as ground truth, e.g. reproducing Fig. 6 protein-mRNA co-expression and CD3E detection-rate analyses. - **cite-seq-protein-rna-integration**: Use when CITE-seq data pairs surface-protein abundance (e.g. CD16/CD56 ADTs) with RNA counts for the same genes and the goal is to test whether RNA denoising recovers protein-defined sub-population structure. - **cite-sources**: Generate properly formatted citations and references from research ([David-Li0406/meta-skill-evloving](https://github.com/David-Li0406/meta-skill-evloving/tree/HEAD/skill-flow/data/skills-refined-36k/skillsmp/cite-sources) · ★5)

- **citevahti-dev**: Use when checking manuscript claims against evidence, assessing whether a citation actually supports what it is cited for, adding audited references to Zotero, generating a citation-integrity report, or when a researcher mentions claims, citations, PubMed, Zotero, PRISMA, blinded rating, or peer review of evidence ([heidihelena/citevahti](https://github.com/heidihelena/citevahti/tree/HEAD/skills/citevahti-dev) · ★1)
- **citevahti-eval**: Use when measuring or publishing CiteVahti's own accuracy — running the automatic claim-lexicon evaluation, checking it against its regression baseline, reasoning about how AI second-rater models are rated, or answering "how accurate is CiteVahti?". Follows ADR-0009's three evaluation objects (automatic lexicon eval, continuous model rating, pooled Atlas). Maintainer-facing; not for checking a researcher's manuscript (that is citevahti-dev). ([heidihelena/citevahti](https://github.com/heidihelena/citevahti/tree/HEAD/skills/citevahti-eval) · ★1)
- **citevahti-models**: Use when choosing or comparing the AI second-rater model in CiteVahti — picking a model, running the same topic through several models to see where they diverge, reading a model's rating/scoreboard standing, acting on a low-rated model, or setting up a multi-model pre-check before a guideline group starts. Follows ADR-0009 (models are cheese-slices: value is complementary catches, not agreement). Not for rating the lexical detector (citevahti-eval) or checking a manuscript (citevahti-dev). ([heidihelena/citevahti](https://github.com/heidihelena/citevahti/tree/HEAD/skills/citevahti-models) · ★1)
- **citevahti-onboarding**: Use when writing or regenerating CiteVahti install and quickstart documentation for a distribution channel — the desktop app, the .mcpb in Claude Desktop, MCP config for Claude Code or other clients, the VS Code extension, or pip — or when checking that channel docs still match the shipped release.
